## Shared source texts

### T04c1fa5240e1b00442d99084

Text hash b05f2814c8550aa8a62025b0dc77ea4e50007fdbf101b712bd2d6d5d99f82551; absolute characters 0–888; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6945-amending-code-fair-competition-for-the-distilled-spirits-rectifying.txt

```text
Whereas the Federal Alcohol Control Administration has submitted for my approval an amendment to the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Rectifying Industry and has rendered its report and recommendations and findings thereon; and

Whereas due notice of public hearings on said proposed amendment has been given and public hearings thereon have been held pursuant to such notice;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act of June 16, 1933 (ch. 90, 48 Stat. 195), and in order to effectuate the purposes of said Title, I hereby approve said amendment and modify my previous approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Rectifying Industry to include an approval of said Code in its entirety as hereby amended.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

January 10, 1935.

```

### T129bf129110b4aec830bff09

Text hash 04b6a5aeecad967f2c5c46d62a6584f589cd8bf833f7f5969901a99b7dd59170; absolute characters 0–1228; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6293-code-fair-competition-for-the-textile-bag-industry.txt

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Textile Bag Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said Code of Fair Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the requirements of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

Approval recommended:

     Hugh S. Johnson.

          Administrator.

The White House,

September 18, 1933.

```

### T23d31101e62c83eced94e50b

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Soap and Glycerine Manufacturing Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said code of fair competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said code of fair competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the requirements of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations, and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said code of fair competition be and is hereby approved.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT,

President.

Approval recommended:

     Hugh S. Johnson.

          Administrator.

The White House,

November 2, 1933.

```

```text
Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations, and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said code of fair competition be and it is hereby approved.
```

### T367aee19b743bc8b1f1af2e8

Text hash 6a41f31ee747e21c59f0702a4c2d39f5e904e190d812cb2352001e0c8c19b61c; absolute characters 0–1975; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6811-approval-amendment-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-distilled.txt

```text
Whereas, the Federal Alcohol Control Administration has submitted for my approval, an Amendment to the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry, and has rendered its report and recommendations and findings thereon:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby find:

1. That an application has been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of the Act and of the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry, for my approval of an Amendment to the said Code; and

2. That due notice and opportunity for hearing to interested parties have been given pursuant to the provisions of the Act and regulations thereunder; and

3. That hearings have been held upon the Amendment pursuant to such notice and the provisions of the Act and regulations thereunder; and

4. That said Code of Fair Competition as hereby amended will constitute a Code of Fair Competition as contemplated by the Act and complies in all respects with pertinent provisions of the Act, including Section 3(a) of Title I thereof; and

5. That it appears, after due consideration, that said Code as hereby amended will tend to effectuate the policy of Congress as declared in Section 1 of Title I of the Act; and

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby approve said Amendment and modify my previous approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry by Executive Order dated November 26, 1933, and my previous approval of the said Code as amended by Executive Order dated June 29, 1934, to include an approval of said Code in its entirety as hereby amended.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

August 4, 1934.

```

### T36936e9769fd2db3ddaf1593

Text hash a8a4694978781688acfa248bc271c2818677af3894d67e56edaf3b6edf2dc676; absolute characters 0–1237; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6531-code-fair-competition-for-the-silverware-manufacturing-industry.txt

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Silverware Manufacturing Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said Code of Fair Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of Title I of said Act and that the requirements of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of Section 3 of the said Act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and is hereby approved.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

Approval recommended:

     Hugh S. Johnson.

          Administrator.

The White House,

December 23, 1933.

```

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Valve and Fittings Manufacturing Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said Code of Fair Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of Title I of said Act and that the requirements of Clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of Section 3 of the said Act have been met:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report and recommendations, and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved, on condition that the filed effective price lists, as shown in Article IV, Section (1), Paragraph (4), shall be open to the inspection of the Trade Factors concerned.

December 15, 1933.
```

```text
Whereas the Federal Alcohol Control Administration has submitted for my approval certain amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry and has rendered its report and recommendations and findings thereon; and

Whereas due notice of a public hearing on said proposed amendments has been given and a public hearing thereon has been held pursuant to such notice;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act of June 16, 1933 (ch. 90, 48 Stat. 195), and in order to effectuate the purposes of said Title, I hereby approve said amendments and modify my previous approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry to include an approval of said Code in its entirety as hereby amended.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

April 5, 1935.

```

```text
Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act of June 16, 1933 (ch. 90, 48 Stat. 195), and in order to effectuate the purposes of said Title, I hereby approve said amendment and modify my previous approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Rectifying Industry to include an approval of said Code in its entirety as hereby amended.
```

### Tc4c2e4d082e57ccb1a454d6e

Text hash 95cc9e06b023b30ca9e1d21545110fc3b2415beea7a91047d95ecf8b8225f4d3; absolute characters 0–1700; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6487-b-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-distilled-spirits-rectifying.txt

```text
Whereas, the Secretary of Agriculture having submitted for my approval a Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Rectifying Industry and having rendered his report and recommendations and findings thereon;

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 10, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby find that:

1. Due notice and opportunity for hearings to interested parties have been given pursuant to the provisions of the act and regulations thereunder, upon a code of fair competition presented by the Secretary of Agriculture upon his own motion pursuant to section 3 (d) of the act, and Executive orders under the act; and,

2. Hearings have been held upon said code, pursuant to such notice and pursuant to the pertinent provisions of the act and regulations thereunder; and,

3. Said code of fair competition constitutes a code of fair competition, as contemplated by the act and complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of the act, including clause (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of title I of the act; and,

4. It appears, after due consideration, that said code of fair competition will tend to effectuate the policy of Congress as declared in section 1 of title I of the act,

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby approve said Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Rectifying Industry.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 9, 1933.

```

### Td6990b35d10caf1d5d52a9a1

Text hash a3e6e48e95fff804f05f47bd23bec0b2f97ddbf64c1d468fbcf0c1a2a7f08a00; absolute characters 0–1738; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6539-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-wine-industry.txt

```text
Whereas, the Secretary of Agriculture having submitted for my approval a Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry and having rendered his report and recommendations and findings thereon:

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby find that:

1. An application has been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with the provisions of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry; and,

2. Due notice and opportunity for hearings to interested parties have been given pursuant to the provisions of the Act and regulations thereunder; and,

3. Hearings have been held upon said Code, pursuant to such notice and pursuant to the pertinent provisions of the Act and regulations thereunder; and,

4. Said Code of Fair Competition constitutes a Code of Fair Competition, as contemplated by the Act and complies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of the Act, including clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of Section 3 of Title I of the Act; and,

5. It appears, after due consideration, that said Code of Fair Competition will tend to effectuate the policy of Congress as declared in Section 1 of Title I of the Act.

Now, Therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby approve said Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 27, 1933.

```

Res. 113 repealed on June 14, 1935; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded only as a validity characterization.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No held record shows this code cancelled, modified under section 10(b) in a way that replaces it, suspended or superseded before June 14, 1935: the census register, the WPA 1944 list and a code-name search of the held order texts (jobs/*/output/texts) were checked, and the machine searches (current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr, later-events:ofr_catalog) have no hit for this order; the OFR catalog starts in 1937 and is not adequate alone, and a code-name search can miss an order that names the code in other words. A held later order, EO 6529 of December 1933, approves modifications of the code under Article VIII, Section (b) of the code and makes them \\\"a part of said Code\\\"; it is an amendment and therefore a later event, not an end, and it does not revoke or cancel the approval. June 16, 1935 is not an end: section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced the two-year expiry with April 1, 1936, and nothing in the resolution defers section 2.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, committee, report direction or other command independent of the code appears; \\\"Approval recommended: Hugh S. and the section 5 exemption for completed action survive only as accrued effects; no held source shows a prosecution or enforcement continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues this approval.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order has one operative command, the approval of the code: \\\"do adopt and approve the report, recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that Res. 113 repealed on June 14, 1935; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded only as a validity characterization.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No held record shows this code cancelled, modified under section 10(b) in a way that replaces it, suspended or superseded before June 14, 1935: the census register, the WPA 1944 list and a code-name search of the held order texts (jobs/*/output/texts) were checked, and the machine searches (current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr, later-events:ofr_catalog) have no hit for this order; the OFR catalog starts in 1937 and is not adequate alone, and a code-name search can miss an order that names the code in other words. Res. 113 replaced the two-year expiry with April 1, 1936, and nothing in the resolution defers section 2.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, committee, report direction or other command independent of the code appears; \\\"Approval recommended: Hugh S. held American Presidency Project transcription of EO:6310 is a one-command order: after reciting the application, hearings and the Administrator's findings under section 3(a) of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, the President wrote \"do order that the said code of fair competition be and it is hereby approved\" for the Code of Fair Competition for the Textile Machinery Manufacturing Industry. It states no period, condition, \"until\" date, provisional approval or exception, so no own-terms end applies. The command depends wholly on section 3 of Title I. Under the reviewed shared analysis, the first end of such a command is the statutory repeal on June 14, 1935 (S.J. Res. 113 section 2, \"All the provisions of Title I of such Act delegating power to the President to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition and providing for the enforcement of such codes are hereby repealed\"; approved June 14, 1935) unless a held record shows an earlier cancellation or suspension, and none is held for this code.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The held corpus was searched for later orders naming the Textile Machinery Manufacturing Industry code or EO:6310: the Cruden lookups for this packet found 0 citations of the order number and 0 mentions of the code name in other held texts, and the register and the 1944 WPA list show only this approval (1933). The current-law USC and eCFR searches found 0 hits; the OFR catalog starts in 1937 and is not adequate on its own for a 1933 order, so it is not relied on. No held record shows this code cancelled, modified or suspended before June 14, 1935. June 16, 1935 is not an end: section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains no appointment, delegation, direction to an agency or other command independent of the code approval. The words \\\"and otherwise\\\" in the authority recital are not a separate command. The adoption of the Administrator's report, recommendations and findings is part of the same approval.\",\"savings_check\":\"Fines, injunctions and acts done under the code before June 14, 1935 are accrued consequences; the antitrust exemption of section 5 for past complying action is an accrued effect (SUPPLEMENT:NIRA5, and the proviso in SUPPLEMENT:SJRES113). None of these continues the approval as an operative command.\",\"scope_check\":\"The command approves the whole Code of Fair Competition for the Textile Machinery Manufacturing Industry under section 3(a) of Title I. The order imposes no exception, condition, committee, stay or report direction, and its text has a single operative paragraph. The one-clause inventory is complete.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"1ff86aaeb65e2ab11ccff6c7f2787330af017c4cf726693e282aa4c3113a45f6","path":"research/excavator/opus-native-rehearsal-2026-10-29/r1/packet/supp/complete-v2/receipts/research/excavator/opus-native-rehearsal-2026-10-29/r1/packet/supp/reviews/AFTER_REVEAL.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld_after_reveal","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"1ff86aaeb65e2ab11ccff6c7f2787330af017c4cf726693e282aa4c3113a45f6","path":"research/excavator/opus-native-rehearsal-2026-10-29/r1/packet/supp/reviews/AFTER_REVEAL.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld_after_reveal","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6310#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6310#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"62b6fa52021acd70c9e97be0a11af01759d121003d1a69cb64154ac578ef0dd9","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6310-code-fair-competition-for-the-textile-machinery-manufacturing.txt","sha256":"62b6fa52021acd70c9e97be0a11af01759d1 Repeal route per the reviewed shared analysis: an approval under section 3 depended on the code approval and enforcement provisions that section 2 of S.J. Res. 113 repealed on June 14, 1935; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded only as a validity characterization.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No held record shows this code cancelled, modified under section 10(b) in a way that replaces it, suspended or superseded before June 14, 1935: the census register, the WPA 1944 list and a code-name search of the held order texts (jobs/*/output/texts) were checked, and the machine searches (current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr, later-events:ofr_catalog) have no hit for this order; the OFR catalog starts in 1937 and is not adequate alone, and a code-name search can miss an order that names the code in other words. Held EO 6204-B (July 14, 1933) earlier approved in part certain provisions of a code for the same industry pending a hearing; it precedes this order and is not an end of it. No held later order names the Throwing Industry code. June 16, 1935 is not an end: section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced the two-year expiry with April 1, 1936, and nothing in the resolution defers section 2.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, committee, report direction or other command independent of the code appears; \\\"Approval recommended: Hugh S. approval.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order has one operative command, the approval of the code: \\\"do adopt and approve the report, recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that Read in full from the held American Presidency Project transcription (page record checked; identity, date and subject confirmed against the census register and the WPA 1944 list); the order's own text states no limit on the approval. Its command depends entirely on the code approval, so its first end is the repeal of the code-approval and code-enforcement provisions of Title I by section 2 of S.J. Res. 113 on June 14, 1935, applying SHARED_ANALYSIS.md and its adjudication; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded as a validity characterization only.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order states no period, \\\"until\\\" date, condition or provisional approval that limits the approval, and no stay or postponement to a fixed date. Held later orders naming the code are EO 6685 (April 20, 1934), EO 6812 (August 4, 1934), EO 6945 (January 10, 1935), EO 6954 (January 31, 1935), EO 7017 (April 19, 1935) and EO 7022 (April 20, 1935). I read the operative sentence of each: each approves amendments and modifies the previous approval to include an approval of the code in its entirety as amended. None cancels, suspends, supersedes or revokes the approval, so each is a later event. No held text cites this order by number. No held record shows this code cancelled, suspended, superseded or revoked before June 14, 1935, and none shows a modification under section 10(b) that reached this order's command. June 16, 1935 is not an end, because section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936. Schechter (May 27, 1935) is a validity characterization, not an end date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, direction to a government agency, report direction or other command independent of the code approval appears. The \\\"Approval recommended\\\" and signature lines command nothing.\",\"savings_check\":\"Read S.J. Res. 113 section 2 and its proviso and NIRA section 5 in the packet excerpts. No held source continues this approval, a prosecution under it or any command of this order after June 14, 1935; accrued acts, liabilities and the section 5 exemption for completed action are recorded as transferred or accrued and not as continued operation.\",\"scope_check\":\"The machine inventory has four clauses, items 1 to 4, which are the President's numbered findings (notice and hearing, hearings held, the code complies with section 3, the code will tend to effectuate the policy of Congress) and, at the end of item 4, the final approval. Findings command nothing. The only operative command is the final sentence approving the code, which is the single corrected clause (inventory correction recorded). The order also says the code was presented \\\"pursuant to section 3 (d) of the act\\\"; the order approves the code under Title I, so the section 3 analysis applies, and a prescription under section 3(d) was equally repealed by section 2 of S.J. Res. 113. The held transcription reads \\\"approved June 10, 1933\\\" in the first operative sentence and \\\"approved June 16, 1933\\\" in the final one; the act is dated June 16, 1933 and the discrepancy does not affect the command.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"641375f97621e76e91baabb3113f7e3b1305681bd9aacffd24c5a84b719aa550","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:9","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"641375f97621e76e91baabb3113f7e3b1305681bd9aacffd24c5a84b719aa550","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:9","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6487B#corrected-1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6487B#corrected-1"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"95cc9e06b023b30ca9e1d21545110fc3b2415beea7a91047d95ecf8b8225f4d3","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6487-b-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-distilled-spirits-rectifying.txt","sha256":"95cc9e06b023b30ca9e1d21545110fc3b2415beea7a91047d95ecf8b8225f4 Johnson, Administrator\\\" is a recommendation, not a command.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings source SUPPLEMENT:SJRES113 (with SUPPLEMENT:NIRA5 for the section 5 text) read; accrued acts, liabilities and the section 5 exemption for completed action survive only as accrued effects; no held source shows a prosecution or enforcement continued after June 14, 1935, and none continues this approval.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order has one operative command, the approval of the code: \\\"do adopt and approve the report and recommendations, and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved, on condition that the filed effective price lists, as shown in Article IV, Section (1), Paragraph (4), shall be open to the inspection of the Trade Factors concerned.\\\" The inventory is a single whole-order clause and was confirmed against the full text; the recitals describe the application, hearings and the Administrator's findings and command nothing. Order 6811 (August 4, 1934) is a approval of an amendment to the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry and modification of the earlier approvals to cover the code as amended under section 3 of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act. Read in full from the held American Presidency Project transcription (page record checked; identity, date and subject confirmed against the census register and the WPA 1944 list); the order's own text states no limit on the approval. Its command depends entirely on the code approval, so its first end is the repeal of the code-approval and code-enforcement provisions of Title I by section 2 of S.J. Res. 113 on June 14, 1935, applying SHARED_ANALYSIS.md and its adjudication; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded as a validity characterization only.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order states no period, \\\"until\\\" date, condition or provisional approval that limits the approval, and no stay or postponement to a fixed date. Held later orders naming the code are EO 7000 (April 5, 1935) and EO 7018 (April 19, 1935); the earlier approvals are EO 6444 (November 26, 1933) and EO 6756-D (June 29, 1934). I read the operative sentence of each: each approves amendments and modifies the previous approval to include an approval of the code in its entirety as amended. None cancels, suspends, supersedes or revokes the approval, so each later order is a later event. No held text cites this order by number. No held record shows this code cancelled, suspended, superseded or revoked before June 14, 1935, and none shows a modification under section 10(b) that reached this order's command. June 16, 1935 is not an end, because section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936. Schechter (May 27, 1935) is a validity characterization, not an end date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, direction to a government agency, report direction or other command independent of the code approval appears. The \\\"Approval recommended\\\" and signature lines command nothing.\",\"savings_check\":\"Read S.J. Res. 113 section 2 and its proviso and NIRA section 5 in the packet excerpts. No held source continues this approval, a prosecution under it or any command of this order after June 14, 1935; accrued acts, liabilities and the section 5 exemption for completed action are recorded as transferred or accrued and not as continued operation.\",\"scope_check\":\"The machine inventory has five clauses, items 1 to 5, which are the President's numbered findings (application made, notice, hearings held, the code as amended complies with section 3(a), the policy of Congress) and, at the end of item 5, the final approval. Findings command nothing. The only operative command is the final sentence approving the amendment and modifying the earlier approvals, which is the single corrected clause (inventory correction recorded). The held text does not reproduce the amendment; its content is not needed because it takes effect only as a term of the code approved under section 3.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"641375f97621e76e91baabb3113f7e3b1305681bd9aacffd24c5a84b719aa550","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:15","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"641375f97621e76e91baabb3113f7e3b1305681bd9aacffd24c5a84b719aa550","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:15","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6811#corrected-1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6811#corrected-1"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6a41f31ee747e21c59f0702a4c2d39f5e904e190d812cb2352001e0c8c19b61c","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6811-approval-amendment-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-distilled.txt","sha256":"6a41f31ee747e21c59f0702a4c2d39f5e904e190d812cb2352001e0c8c19b61c","state":" Read in full from the held American Presidency Project transcription (page record checked; identity, date and subject confirmed against the census register and the WPA 1944 list); the order's own text states no limit on the approval. Its command depends entirely on the code approval, so its first end is the repeal of the code-approval and code-enforcement provisions of Title I by section 2 of S.J. Res. 113 on June 14, 1935, applying SHARED_ANALYSIS.md and its adjudication; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded as a validity characterization only.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order states no period, \\\"until\\\" date, condition or provisional approval that limits the approval, and no stay or postponement to a fixed date. Held orders naming the code before this order are EO 6487-B, 6685 and 6812; after it, EO 6954 (January 31, 1935), EO 7017 (April 19, 1935) and EO 7022 (April 20, 1935). I read the operative sentence of each: each approves amendments and modifies the previous approval to include an approval of the code in its entirety as amended. None cancels, suspends, supersedes or revokes the approval, so the later orders are later events. No held text cites this order by number. No held record shows this code cancelled, suspended, superseded or revoked before June 14, 1935, and none shows a modification under section 10(b) that reached this order's command. June 16, 1935 is not an end, because section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936. Schechter (May 27, 1935) is a validity characterization, not an end date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, direction to a government agency, report direction or other command independent of the code approval appears. The \\\"Approval recommended\\\" and signature lines command nothing.\",\"savings_check\":\"Read S.J. Res. 113 section 2 and its proviso and NIRA section 5 in the packet excerpts. No held source continues this approval, a prosecution under it or any command of this order after June 14, 1935; accrued acts, liabilities and the section 5 exemption for completed action are recorded as transferred or accrued and not as continued operation.\",\"scope_check\":\"One operative command: the approval stated in the quoted sentence. The recitals (application, hearings, the Administrator's report and findings) command nothing, and the order reproduces no code text, which is not needed because the approval is the only source of the code's legal effect. The single machine clause covers the whole command.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"641375f97621e76e91baabb3113f7e3b1305681bd9aacffd24c5a84b719aa550","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"641375f97621e76e91baabb3113f7e3b1305681bd9aacffd24c5a84b719aa550","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n03/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6945#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6945#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"b05f2814c8550aa8a62025b0dc77ea4e50007fdbf101b712bd2d6d5d99f82551","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6945-amending-code-fair-competition-for-the-distilled-spirits-rectifying.txt","sha256":"b05f2814c8550aa8a62025b0dc77ea4e50007fdbf101b712bd2d6d5d99f82551","state":"m Order 7000 (April 5, 1935) is a approval of amendments to the Code of Fair Competition for the Distilled Spirits Industry and modification of the earlier approval to cover the code as amended under section 3 of Title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act. Read in full from the held American Presidency Project transcription (page record checked; identity, date and subject confirmed against the census register and the WPA 1944 list); the order's own text states no limit on the approval. Its command depends entirely on the code approval, so its first end is the repeal of the code-approval and code-enforcement provisions of Title I by section 2 of S.J. Res. 113 on June 14, 1935, applying SHARED_ANALYSIS.md and its adjudication; Schechter (May 27, 1935) is recorded as a validity characterization only.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order states no period, \\\"until\\\" date, condition or provisional approval that limits the approval, and no stay or postponement to a fixed date. Held orders naming the code before this order are EO 6444, 6756-D and 6811; after it, EO 7018 (April 19, 1935). I read the operative sentence of each: each approves the code or amendments and modifies the previous approval to include an approval of the code in its entirety as amended. None cancels, suspends, supersedes or revokes the approval, so EO 7018 is a later event. No held text cites this order by number. No held record shows this code cancelled, suspended, superseded or revoked before June 14, 1935, and none shows a modification under section 10(b) that reached this order's command. June 16, 1935 is not an end, because section 1 of S.J. Res. 113 replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936. Schechter (May 27, 1935) is a validity characterization, not an end date.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No appointment, delegation, direction to a government agency, report direction or other command independent of the code approval appears. The \\\"Approval recommended\\\" and signature lines command nothing.\",\"savings_check\":\"Read S.J. Res. 113 section 2 and its proviso and NIRA section 5 in the packet excerpts. No held source continues this approval, a prosecution under it or any command of this order after June 14, 1935; accrued acts, liabilities and the section 5 exemption for completed action are recorded as transferred or accrued and not as continued operation.\",\"scope_check\":\"One operative command: the approval stated in the quoted sentence. The recitals (application, hearings, the Administrator's report and findings) command nothing, and the order reproduces no code text, which is not needed because the approval is the only source of the code's legal effect. Roosevelt) approves the Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry under title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act. The held text has an introductory 'Whereas' paragraph (the Secretary of Agriculture's submission, report, recommendations and findings), a findings paragraph with five numbered findings (application, notice and hearings, compliance with section 3(a) clauses (1) and (2), and tendency to effectuate the policy of section 1), and one operative paragraph ending 'do hereby approve said Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry.' The machine inventory lists the five numbered findings as five clauses (the fifth span also swallows the operative paragraph and the signature line); findings command nothing, so I replaced it with one clause, characters 1402 to 1676, the operative approval sentence (inventory correction recorded). Read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6539-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-wine-industry.txt, grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; 1,738 characters). APP page check (AGENT_PROTOCOL.md, working-text paragraph): the jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with sha256 869906b7aeaa2827a73b7c337d75455e2e66975ed00a8799f2c14b4007564f81 is headed 'Executive Order 6539-A—Approval of Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry', dated December 27, 1933, node app:node/373337, Common Crawl capture 2025-10-15T14:33:52Z; its text_sha256 a3e6e48e95fff804f05f47bd23bec0b2f97ddbf64c1d468fbcf0c1a2a7f08a00 equals the sha256 I computed for the held file, and the page heading (which names the number), the date and the subject agree with the text. Cotton holds the exact page bytes (jobs/cotton-capture-custody/output/captures). Identity also agrees with the census register row and the WPA 1944 list (page 560: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry ap proved.'). Legal basis for the first end. An approval of a code under section 3 of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act had legal effect only through section 3: under 3(b) the code's provisions became the standards of fair competition for the trade or industry and a violation in or affecting interstate or foreign commerce was an unfair method of competition under the Federal Trade Commission Act, under 3(c) the district courts could restrain violations and district attorneys were directed to sue, and under 3(f) each violation was a misdemeanor (the section 3 excerpt held in this packet). Section 2 of S.J. Res. 113, approved June 14, 1935 (49 Stat. 375), repealed 'All the provisions of Title I of such Act delegating power to the President to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition and providing for the enforcement of such codes' and states no effective date other than its approval. That repeal removed the power the approval rested on and every provision through which the approved code was enforced, which is the reason (not a default date) that it ends the prospective operative effect of this command on June 14, 1935. Schechter Poultry Corp. v. United States, 295 U.S. 495 (decided May 27, 1935), held the section 3 code-making delegation unconstitutional ('we hold the code provisions here in question to be invalid'); the held excerpts record that as a validity characterization (an approval under an unconstitutional delegation was void from issuance under the doctrine then prevailing, and May 27, 1935 is when that was declared), not as an end date, because the judgment cancelled no other approving order and a later doctrine treats a measure's earlier existence as an operative fact. June 16, 1935 is not an end: section 1 of the resolution replaced the two-year expiry in section 2(c) with April 1, 1936, and nothing in the resolution defers section 2.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Searches run on 2026-10-31 over the held texts (jobs/*/output/texts): (1) every held order text folder for 'Wine Industry' and 'wine code' (Cruden); (2) the Cruden lookup of this order's number (no held text cites Executive Order 6539 or 6539-A by number); (3) the packet LOOKUPS code-name mentions. Only two held orders name the code: EO 6900 (November 8, 1934) and EO 7020 (April 19, 1935), each of which approves amendments submitted by the Federal Alcohol Control Administration and 'modify my previous approval' of the code to include approval of the code as amended; amendments are later events, not ends, and neither cancels, suspends or replaces the code. EO 6829 (August 21, 1934) delegates to the Federal Alcohol Control Administration functions over alcoholic-beverage industries and reserves to the President 'the power to approve any such code of fair competition or amendment thereof'; it neither cancels nor suspends this approval. The service-trades suspension order of May 26, 1934 reaches only trades the Administrator later designates, and no held record designates the wine industry. The machine searches of the U.S. Code and eCFR (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. No held record shows this code cancelled, modified under section 10(b) so as to end the approval, superseded, suspended or stayed before June 14, 1935. EO 7075 (June 15, 1935), which terminated the National Industrial Recovery Board and created the office of Administrator of the NRA, post-dates the repeal, acts under Title I 'as amended by' the resolution and does not continue a code approval; it and EO 7252 (December 21, 1935, effective January 1, 1936) concern administration. June 16, 1935 is not an end, because section 1 of the resolution replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936. The dossier chain has no located change provision for this order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The only command is the approval of the Code of Fair Competition for the Wine Industry; the Secretary's report and the findings are recitals on which the approval rests. The order makes no appointment, creates no committee or board, delegates nothing under another statute and directs no agency, so no independent command is retained.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts done and liabilities incurred under the code before June 14, 1935 are completed (accrued) effects of the approval; the general savings statute of the time, which ordinarily preserves liabilities incurred under a repealed statute, is not a held source, and the repeal text contains no savings clause. Section 5 of title I exempted from the antitrust laws any code approved and in effect under the title and any action complying with it taken during the period; the resolution's proviso limits that exemption to agreements and action thereunder of two stated kinds and states no time limit, so the held text does not settle whether it narrowed the exemption for action taken under codes before June 14, 1935. Whatever exemption or liability survives for past action is an accrued effect of completed acts, not continued operation of this approval. No held source shows a prosecution, injunction or other proceeding under this code continuing after June 14, 1935, and nothing continues the approval itself.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order approves one code as a whole and attaches no condition, stay, exception, exemption or time limit; the held text states none and no partial event is held or found. The dossier lists no own-terms lead for this order. The words 'and otherwise' in the authority recital add no separate command.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"c09c9d41eccf824f399e7734d2748ce6b7b51c62988bf3c6a9f95bda41f28b87","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n09/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n09/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"c09c9d41eccf824f399e7734d2748ce6b7b51c62988bf3c6a9f95bda41f28b87","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/n09/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6539A#corrected-1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6539A#corrected-1"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60","state":"match"},"sha256":"98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/98e35c567ea86208eadc55548eb7a5ea5f1edd92d8116c9e4c4847da1bab0305/d19d25fe35e98a1ade9b1fd03ed8751743502c5d1005a61ca97974657cf08386/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"0e3a91dad60f57ec5703e3689a4a55dc5ca798db96ec0f1f7bb75b76306e3b60"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"a3e6e48e95fff804f05f47bd23bec0b2f97ddbf64c1d468fbcf0c1a2a7f08a00","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6539-approval-code-fair-competition-for-the-wine-industry.txt","sha256":"a3e6e48e95fff804f05f47bd23bec0b2f97ddbf64c1d468fbcf0c1a2a7f08a00","state":"matc
